\usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_5)=1.54+0.55-0.55=1.54.$$\end{document}Applying equation (3) yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} FRI(\mathfrak {G})&= \sum _{\mathfrak {uv}\in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}} \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u})r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v})r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v})\bigr ] \\&= \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_1)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_1) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_2)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_2)\bigr ] + \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_1)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_1) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)\bigr ] \\&\quad + \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_2)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_2) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_3)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_3)\bigr ] + \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_2)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_2) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)\bigr ] \\&\quad + \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_3)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_3) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)\bigr ] + \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_3)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_3) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_5)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_5)\bigr ] \\&\quad + \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_4) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_5)r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}_5)\bigr ] \\&= \bigl [(0.40)(1.54) + (0.60)(1.03)\bigr ] + \bigl [(0.40)(1.54) + (0.77)(0.55)\bigr ] \\&\quad + \bigl [(0.60)(1.03) + (0.60)(1.03)\bigr ] + \bigl [(0.60)(1.03) + (0.77)(0.55)\bigr ] \\&\quad + \bigl [(0.60)(1.03) + (0.77)(0.55)\bigr ] + \bigl [(0.60)(1.03) + (0.40)(1.54)\bigr ] \\&\quad + \bigl [(0.77)(0.55) + (0.40)(1.54)\bigr ] \\&= 7.8660. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Thus, the fuzzy Revan index for this graph is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G})=7.8660$$\end{document}.

Upper bounds for fuzzy Revan index

We now derive the upper bounds of the fuzzy Revan index for some basic fuzzy graph families. Such theoretical estimates are important for setting benchmarks to understand the role of structural properties and membership constraints in determining FRI values in practical settings.

Theorem 4.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {G} = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {G}})$$\end{document} be a connected fuzzy graph with vertex set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}, vertex membership function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}, and edge membership function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}. Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n = |\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}|$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document} be the edge set. The fuzzy Revan index satisfies the inequality\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G}) \le n^2 \sum _{\mathfrak {uv} \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) \right] .$$\end{document}

Proof

For any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {u} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u})$$\end{document} denote its fuzzy Revan degree. By definition,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) - d_f(\mathfrak {u}),$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) = \max _{\mathfrak {v} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}} d_f(\mathfrak {v})$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) = \min _{\mathfrak {v} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}} d_f(\mathfrak {v})$$\end{document} are the maximum and minimum fuzzy degrees, respectively.

Since each fuzzy degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {u})$$\end{document} is the sum of edge memberships incident to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {u}$$\end{document}, and there are at most n-1 such edges with each membership value lying in [0, 1], we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {u}) \le n-1 \quad \text {for all } \mathfrak {u} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}.$$\end{document}Consequently,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) \le n-1 \quad \text {and} \quad \delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) \ge 0.$$\end{document}Now, for any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {u} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {G}) - d_f(\mathfrak {u}) \le (n-1) + (n-1) - 0 = 2n-2.$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2n-2 \le n^2$$\end{document} for all integers n \ge 1, it follows that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) \le n^2 \quad \text {for every } \mathfrak {u} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}.$$\end{document}Because vertex membership values satisfy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) \le 1$$\end{document} for all \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {u} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}, multiplying the above inequality by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u})$$\end{document} preserves the direction:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) \le \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) n^2,$$\end{document}and similarly,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) \le \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) n^2,$$\end{document}for any adjacent pair \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {u}, \mathfrak {v} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document}.

Summing these inequalities over all edges \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {uv} \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}$$\end{document} yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{\mathfrak {uv} \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) r_{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) \right] \le n^2 \sum _{\mathfrak {uv} \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) \right] .$$\end{document}By the definition of the fuzzy Revan index given in Equation 3, the left-hand side is exactly \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G})$$\end{document}. Therefore,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G}) \le n^2 \sum _{\mathfrak {uv} \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}}} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {u}) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}}(\mathfrak {v}) \right] .$$\end{document}This completes the proof. □

Theorem 4.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {P}_n = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {P}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {P}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {P}})$$\end{document} be a fuzzy path graph with n vertices \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_1, \mathfrak {v}_2, \ldots , \mathfrak {v}_n$$\end{document} and edge set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {P}} = \{\mathfrak {v}_1\mathfrak {v}_2, \mathfrak {v}_2\mathfrak {v}_3, \ldots , \mathfrak {v}_{n-1}\mathfrak {v}_n\}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan index satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le 2n.$$\end{document}

Proof

Consider a fuzzy path graph denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {P}(\mathfrak {v}_1, \mathfrak {v}_2, \dots , \mathfrak {v}_n)$$\end{document}. Then, the fuzzy degrees are\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_1) = \nu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_1\mathfrak {v}_2), \quad d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \nu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i-1}\mathfrak {v}_i) + \nu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \;\; (2 \le i \le n-1), \quad d_f(\mathfrak {v}_n) = \nu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_{n-1}\mathfrak {v}_n).$$\end{document}The fuzzy Revan index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n)$$\end{document} can then be bounded by the following summation:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) + \mu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \right] ,$$\end{document}Given that the membership values for vertices, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}, and edges, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_{i+1})$$\end{document}, are constrained to the interval [0, 1], the Revan degree for any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_i$$\end{document} is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {P}_n) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {P}_n) - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i),$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f(\mathfrak {P}_n) = \max \{d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i): \mathfrak {v}_i \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {P}}\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f(\mathfrak {P}_n) = \min \{d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i): \mathfrak {v}_i \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {P}}\}$$\end{document}.

By substituting this expression for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document} into the summation for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n)$$\end{document} and noting that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 1$$\end{document}, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ (\Delta _f + \delta _f - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)) + (\Delta _f + \delta _f - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1})) \right] .$$\end{document}This simplifies the terms within the summation to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ 2(\Delta _f + \delta _f) - \left( d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) + d_f(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \right) \right] .$$\end{document}In a fuzzy path graph, each edge membership satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \nu _{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \le 1$$\end{document}, which implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_1) \le 1$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_n) \le 1$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 2$$\end{document} for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2 \le i \le n-1$$\end{document}. Consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le 2$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}. The maximum contribution of each pair of terms within the sum occurs when \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f + \delta _f$$\end{document} is maximized and the degree terms are minimized. Taking \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f = 2$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f = 0$$\end{document}, and noting that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) + d_f(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \ge 0$$\end{document}, each bracketed term is at most 2(2+0) = 4.

Summing these maximum contributions across all n-1 edges in the path graph yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le 4(n-1).$$\end{document}However, a tighter bound can be obtained using a direct expansion. Expanding the sum gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) + r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \right] = r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_1) + 2\sum _{i=2}^{n-1} r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) + r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_n).$$\end{document}Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {P}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \Delta _f + \delta _f - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 2$$\end{document} for all vertices (as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le 2$$\end{document}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \ge 0$$\end{document}), we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le 2 + 2(n-2) + 2 = 2n.$$\end{document}Thus, we obtain the desired inequality as follows\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {P}_n) \le 2n.$$\end{document}□

Theorem 4.3

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {C}_n = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {C}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {C}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {C}})$$\end{document} be a fuzzy cycle graph with n vertices \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_1, \mathfrak {v}_2, \ldots , \mathfrak {v}_n$$\end{document} and edge set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {C}} = \{\mathfrak {v}_1\mathfrak {v}_2, \mathfrak {v}_2\mathfrak {v}_3, \ldots , \mathfrak {v}_{n-1}\mathfrak {v}_n, \mathfrak {v}_n\mathfrak {v}_1\}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan index satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n) \le 4n.$$\end{document}

Proof

Consider a fuzzy cycle graph denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {C}(\mathfrak {v}_1, \mathfrak {v}_2, \dots , \mathfrak {v}_n)$$\end{document}. The fuzzy degrees of the vertices are given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \nu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i-1}\mathfrak {v}_i) + \nu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \quad (1 \le i \le n),$$\end{document}with indices considered modulo n ensuring that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_0 = \mathfrak {v}_n$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_{n+1} = \mathfrak {v}_1$$\end{document}.

The fuzzy Revan index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n)$$\end{document} can then be bounded by the following summation:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) + \mu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \right] ,$$\end{document}where, owing to the cyclic nature of the graph, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_{n+1} = \mathfrak {v}_1$$\end{document}.

Given that the membership values for vertices, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}, are constrained to the interval [0, 1], the Revan degree for any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_i$$\end{document} is defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {C}_n) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {C}_n) - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i),$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f(\mathfrak {C}_n) = \max _i d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f(\mathfrak {C}_n) = \min _i d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}.

By substituting this expression for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document} into the summation for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n)$$\end{document} and noting that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 1$$\end{document}, we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n} \left[ (\Delta _f + \delta _f - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)) + (\Delta _f + \delta _f - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1})) \right] .$$\end{document}This reduces to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n} \left[ 2(\Delta _f + \delta _f) - \left( d_f(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) + d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \right) \right] .$$\end{document}Expanding the left-hand side yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sum _{i=1}^{n} \left[ r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i}) + r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \right] = \sum _{i=1}^{n} 2 r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i}).$$\end{document}In a fuzzy cycle graph, each edge membership satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \nu _{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \le 1$$\end{document}, thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 2$$\end{document} for all i. Hence, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le 2$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}, yielding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {C}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 2$$\end{document} for all vertices. Consequently,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {C}_n) \le 2\sum _{i=1}^{n} 2 = 4n,$$\end{document}This establishes the desired result. □

Theorem 4.4

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {K}_n = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {K}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {K}})$$\end{document} be a fuzzy complete graph with n vertices where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {K}} = \{\mathfrak {u}\mathfrak {v}: \mathfrak {u}, \mathfrak {v} \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {K}}, \mathfrak {u} \ne \mathfrak {v}\}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan index satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_n) \le n(n-1)^2.$$\end{document}

Proof

Consider a complete fuzzy graph denoted as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {K}_n$$\end{document} with vertex set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {K}} = \{\mathfrak {v}_1, \mathfrak {v}_2, \dots , \mathfrak {v}_n\}$$\end{document}. In a complete graph, every vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_i$$\end{document} is adjacent to all other n-1 vertices, so the fuzzy degree of each vertex is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \sum _{\begin{array}{c} j=1 \\ j \ne i \end{array}}^{n} \nu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_j) \quad (1 \le i \le n),$$\end{document}Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_n) = \max _i d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_n) = \min _i d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan degree of vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_i$$\end{document} is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_n) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_n) - d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i).$$\end{document}The fuzzy Revan index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_n)$$\end{document} can be bounded by summing over all edges as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_n) \le \sum _{i=1}^{n} \sum _{j=i+1}^{n} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) + \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_j) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_j) \right] .$$\end{document}Because vertex memberships satisfy \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}. Moreover, each edge membership satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \nu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_j) \le 1$$\end{document}, thus \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le n-1$$\end{document} for all i. Consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le n-1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}, yielding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le n-1$$\end{document} for all vertices.

A complete graph has \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\frac{n(n-1)}{2}$$\end{document} edges. Substituting these bounds gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_n) \le \frac{n(n-1)}{2} \cdot \left[ 2(n-1)\right] = \frac{n(n-1)}{2} \cdot 2(n-1) = n(n-1)^2,$$\end{document}This establishes the desired result. □

Theorem 4.5

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {W}_n = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {W}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {W}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {W}})$$\end{document} be a fuzzy wheel graph with n \ge 4 vertices, consisting of a central hub \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_h$$\end{document} and n-1 rim vertices \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_1, \ldots , \mathfrak {v}_{n-1}$$\end{document} arranged in a cycle. The fuzzy Revan index satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {W}_n) \le 4(n-1)^2.$$\end{document}

Proof

Consider a fuzzy wheel graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {W}_n$$\end{document} consisting of a central hub vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_h$$\end{document} and n-1 rim vertices \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_1, \mathfrak {v}_2, \dots , \mathfrak {v}_{n-1}$$\end{document} arranged in a cycle. The hub is connected to every rim vertex, and consecutive rim vertices are adjacent to each other.

The fuzzy degrees are given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_h) = \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \nu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_h\mathfrak {v}_i), \qquad d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \nu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_h\mathfrak {v}_i) + \nu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_i\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) + \nu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i-1}\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$1 \le i \le n-1$$\end{document}, with indices taken modulo n-1, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_0 = \mathfrak {v}_{n-1}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_n = \mathfrak {v}_1$$\end{document}. Fuzzy Revan degree of any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {u}$$\end{document} is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {u}) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {W}_n) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {W}_n) - d_f(\mathfrak {u}).$$\end{document}Since each edge membership satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \nu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\cdot ) \le 1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {v}_h) \le n-1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 3$$\end{document} for all rim vertices. Consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le n-1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}, yielding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_h) \le n-1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le n-1$$\end{document} for all vertices.

The fuzzy Revan index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {W}_n)$$\end{document} is obtained by summing over all edges, including n-1 hub-rim edges and n-1 rim edges:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {W}_n) = \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_h)r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_h) + \mu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) \right] + \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_i) + \mu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1})r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {v}_{i+1}) \right] ,$$\end{document}where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_{n} = \mathfrak {v}_1$$\end{document} in the second summation.

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\cdot ) \le 1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {u})r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {u}) \le r_{\mathfrak {W}}(\mathfrak {u}) \le n-1$$\end{document} for every vertex. Thus, each hub-rim edge contributes at most (n-1) + (n-1) = 2(n-1), and similarly, each rim edge contributes at most 2(n-1).

There are n-1 hub-rim edges and n-1 rim edges, giving a total of 2(n-1) edges. Summing these maximum contributions yields\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {W}_n) \le 2(n-1) \cdot 2(n-1) = 4(n-1)^2.$$\end{document}Therefore, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {W}_n) \le 4(n-1)^2$$\end{document}, which completes the proof. □

Theorem 4.6

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {S}_{n} = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {S}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {S}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {S}})$$\end{document} be a fuzzy star graph consisting of a central hub vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_{h}$$\end{document} and n-1 leaf vertices \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}_{1}, \mathfrak {v}_{2}, \ldots , \mathfrak {v}_{n-1}$$\end{document}, with edge set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {S}} = \{\mathfrak {v}_{h}\mathfrak {v}_{i}: 1 \le i \le n-1\}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan index of this star graph satisfies:4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} FRI(\mathfrak {S}_{n}) \le 2(n-1)^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Proof

For a star graph, the hub connects to all n-1 leaves. The fuzzy degree of the hub is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_h) = \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \nu _{\mathfrak {S}}(\mathfrak {v}_h\mathfrak {v}_i) \le n-1$$\end{document}. Each leaf has degree \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i) = \nu _{\mathfrak {S}}(\mathfrak {v}_h\mathfrak {v}_i) \le 1$$\end{document}. Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le n-1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}.

The fuzzy Revan degree of any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}$$\end{document} is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r(\mathfrak {v}) = \Delta _f + \delta _f - d_f(\mathfrak {v})$$\end{document}.

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {v}_h) \ge \delta _f$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \le d_f(\mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}, we obtain \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r(\mathfrak {v}_h) \le \Delta _f \le n-1$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r(\mathfrak {v}_i) \le \Delta _f \le n-1$$\end{document} for all leaves.

Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu _{\mathfrak {S}}(\cdot ) \le 1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {S}}(\mathfrak {v})r(\mathfrak {v}) \le r(\mathfrak {v}) \le n-1$$\end{document} for every vertex. For each edge \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(\mathfrak {v}_h, \mathfrak {v}_i)$$\end{document}, the contribution is at most (n-1) + (n-1) = 2(n-1). There are n-1 edges in the star graph. Summing over all edges:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {S}_n) = \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {S}}(\mathfrak {v}_h)r(\mathfrak {v}_h) + \mu _{\mathfrak {S}}(\mathfrak {v}_i)r(\mathfrak {v}_i) \right] \le (n-1) \cdot 2(n-1) = 2(n-1)^2.$$\end{document}Thus, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {S}_n) \le 2(n-1)^2$$\end{document}. □

Theorem 4.7

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {K}_{m,n} = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {K}}, \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}, \nu _{\mathfrak {K}})$$\end{document} be a fuzzy complete bipartite graph with bipartition sets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\mathcal {X}| = m$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$|\mathcal {Y}| = n$$\end{document}, and edge set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {K}} = \{\mathfrak {x}\mathfrak {y}: \mathfrak {x} \in \mathcal {X}, \mathfrak {y} \in \mathcal {Y}\}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan index satisfies\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) \le m^2 n + mn^2.$$\end{document}

Proof

Consider a complete bipartite fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}$$\end{document} with vertex sets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X} = \{\mathfrak {x}_1, \mathfrak {x}_2, \dots , \mathfrak {x}_m\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y} = \{\mathfrak {y}_1, \mathfrak {y}_2, \dots , \mathfrak {y}_n\}$$\end{document}, in which each vertex in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document} is connected to every vertex in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y}$$\end{document}.

Fuzzy degrees are given by\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$d_f(\mathfrak {x}_i) = \sum _{j=1}^{n} \nu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i\mathfrak {y}_j) \quad (1 \le i \le m), \qquad d_f(\mathfrak {y}_j) = \sum _{i=1}^{m} \nu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i\mathfrak {y}_j) \quad (1 \le j \le n).$$\end{document}Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) = \max \{d_f(\mathfrak {x}_i), d_f(\mathfrak {y}_j)\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) = \min \{d_f(\mathfrak {x}_i), d_f(\mathfrak {y}_j)\}$$\end{document}. The fuzzy Revan degree of any vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {v}$$\end{document} is\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {v}) = \Delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) + \delta _f(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) - d_f(\mathfrak {v}).$$\end{document}Since each edge membership satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \nu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i\mathfrak {y}_j) \le 1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {x}_i) \le n$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le d_f(\mathfrak {y}_j) \le m$$\end{document} for all i, j. Consequently, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Delta _f \le \max \{m,n\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\delta _f \ge 0$$\end{document}, yielding \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) \le \max \{m,n\}$$\end{document} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) \le \max \{m,n\}$$\end{document}.

The fuzzy Revan index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n})$$\end{document} is obtained by summing over all mn edges as follows:\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) = \sum _{i=1}^{m} \sum _{j=1}^{n} \left[ \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) + \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) \right] .$$\end{document}Because \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$0 \le \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\cdot ) \le 1$$\end{document}, we have \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) \le r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) \le \max \{m,n\}$$\end{document} and similarly \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) \le r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) \le \max \{m,n\}$$\end{document}. Thus, each edge contributes at most \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$2\max \{m,n\}$$\end{document}.

A tighter bound can be obtained by noting that vertices in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X}$$\end{document} have fuzzy degree at most n, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) \le n$$\end{document}, and vertices in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y}$$\end{document} have fuzzy degree at most m, so \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) \le m$$\end{document}. Hence,\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {x}_i) + \mu _{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) r_{\mathfrak {K}}(\mathfrak {y}_j) \le n + m.$$\end{document}Summing over all mn edges gives\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {K}_{m,n}) \le mn(m + n) = m^2 n + m n^2,$$\end{document}which establishes the desired inequality. □

Computational complexity

Algorithms 1 and 2 each run in O(n + |E|) time, where n is the number of vertices and |E| is the number of edges. Vertex deletion analysis requires running Algorithm 2 for each of the n vertices, resulting in O(n|E|) total time. For sparse graphs with |E| = O(n), this simplifies to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(n^2)$$\end{document}. The CRITIC method (Algorithm 3) operates on an m × n decision matrix with m alternatives and n criteria, requiring \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$O(mn + n^2 m)$$\end{document} times. In our highway site selection case study with m=6 and n=5, the computational cost was negligible.

Integrated FRI-CRITIC methodology for site selection

We now present a comprehensive algorithm that first computes the fuzzy Revan index for fuzzy graphs and then integrates it with the CRITIC method to establish objective criteria weights for multi-criteria decision-making11. This integrated approach was used to assess and prioritize the site locations.

A fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {G}_j = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j},\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j})$$\end{document} for each criterion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \begin{document}$$Y_j$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} 0$$\end{document} for cost criteria.

A fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {G}_j = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j},\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j})$$\end{document} for each criterion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y_j$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j} = \{\mathfrak {s}_1,\mathfrak {s}_2,\ldots ,\mathfrak {s}_6\}$$\end{document} represents the vertices (site locations), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j}$$\end{document} designates the set of edges with corresponding membership values \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{\mathfrak {G}_j}(\mathfrak {s}_p\mathfrak {s}_q)$$\end{document}.

A fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {G}_j = (\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j},\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j})$$\end{document} for each criterion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y_j$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j} = \{\mathfrak {s}_1,\mathfrak {s}_2,\ldots ,\mathfrak {s}_6\}$$\end{document} represents the vertices (site locations), and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j}$$\end{document} designates the set of edges with corresponding membership values \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{\mathfrak {G}_j}(\mathfrak {s}_p\mathfrak {s}_q)$$\end{document}.
