The decision matrix \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\text {FRI}(S_{ij})$$\end{document} illustrating the FRI values for location \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$S_i$$\end{document} based on criterion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y_j$$\end{document}, where i = 1,2,… ,6 and j = 1,2,… ,5.

The benefit-cost indicator vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{b} = (b_1,b_2,\ldots ,b_5)$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b_j = 1$$\end{document} for benefit criteria and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b_j = 0$$\end{document} for cost criteria.

The benefit-cost indicator vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{b} = (b_1,b_2,\ldots ,b_5)$$\end{document}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b_j = 1$$\end{document} for benefit criteria and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$b_j = 0$$\end{document} for cost criteria.

Output:

Best site location among \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\mathfrak {s}_1,\mathfrak {s}_2,\ldots ,\mathfrak {s}_6\}$$\end{document}.The overall fuzzy Revan index \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G}_j)$$\end{document} for each criterion graph. Compute vertex degrees: For each vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_i \in \mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j}$$\end{document} in the fuzzy graph \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {G}_j$$\end{document}, calculate its fuzzy degree: 5\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} d_f(\mathfrak {s}_i) = \sum _{\mathfrak {s}_i\mathfrak {s}_k \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j}} \nu _{\mathfrak {G}_j}(\mathfrak {s}_i\mathfrak {s}_k). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Construct vertex-deleted subgraphs: For each vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} Compute mean and standard deviation: For each criterion j, calculate the mean and standard deviation of normalized values: 9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \bar{f}_j = \frac{1}{6}\sum _{i=1}^{6} \tilde{f}_{ij}, \qquad \sigma _j = \sqrt{\frac{1}{5}\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{ij} - \bar{f}_j)^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Compute mean and standard deviation: For each criterion j, calculate the mean and standard deviation of normalized values: 9\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \bar{f}_j = \frac{1}{6}\sum _{i=1}^{6} \tilde{f}_{ij}, \qquad \sigma _j = \sqrt{\frac{1}{5}\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{ij} - \bar{f}_j)^2}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Calculate correlation coefficients: Determine the correlation coefficient \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{jt}$$\end{document} for every combination of criteria j and t: 10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} r_{jt} = \frac{\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{ij} - \bar{f}_j)(\tilde{f}_{it} - \bar{f}_t)}{\sqrt{\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{ij} - \bar{f}_j)^2}\sqrt{\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{it} - \bar{f}_t)^2}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Calculate correlation coefficients: Determine the correlation coefficient \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$r_{jt}$$\end{document} for every combination of criteria j and t: 10\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} r_{jt} = \frac{\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{ij} - \bar{f}_j)(\tilde{f}_{it} - \bar{f}_t)}{\sqrt{\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{ij} - \bar{f}_j)^2}\sqrt{\sum _{i=1}^{6} (\tilde{f}_{it} - \bar{f}_t)^2}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Compute CRITIC indices: Determine the information content (CRITIC index) for each criterion: 11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} c_j = \sigma _j \sum _{t=1}^{5} (1 - r_{jt}). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Compute CRITIC indices: Determine the information content (CRITIC index) for each criterion: 11\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} c_j = \sigma _j \sum _{t=1}^{5} (1 - r_{jt}). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Determine objective weights: Calculate the objective weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_j$$\end{document} for each criterion: 12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w_j = \frac{c_j}{\sum _{k=1}^{5} c_k}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Determine objective weights: Calculate the objective weight \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_j$$\end{document} for each criterion: 12\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w_j = \frac{c_j}{\sum _{k=1}^{5} c_k}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Compute overall scores: Evaluate the overall score for each site location \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_i$$\end{document}: 13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {S}(\mathfrak {s}_i) = \sum _{j=1}^{5} w_j \, f_{ij}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{ij} = FRI_{Y_j}(\mathfrak {s}_i)$$\end{document}.

Compute overall scores: Evaluate the overall score for each site location \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_i$$\end{document}: 13\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {S}(\mathfrak {s}_i) = \sum _{j=1}^{5} w_j \, f_{ij}, \end{aligned}$$\end{document} where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{ij} = FRI_{Y_j}(\mathfrak {s}_i)$$\end{document}.

Compute normalized ranks: Calculate the normalized rank for each site location: 14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_i) = \frac{\mathcal {S}(\mathfrak {s}_i)}{\max _k \mathcal {S}(\mathfrak {s}_k)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Compute normalized ranks: Calculate the normalized rank for each site location: 14\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_i) = \frac{\mathcal {S}(\mathfrak {s}_i)}{\max _k \mathcal {S}(\mathfrak {s}_k)}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}

Rank site locations: Compare all site locations \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_i$$\end{document} with respect to their normalized ranks. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_p)> \mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_q)$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_p \succ \mathfrak {s}_q$$\end{document}, meaning that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_p$$\end{document} is preferred over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_q$$\end{document}.

Rank site locations: Compare all site locations \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_i$$\end{document} with respect to their normalized ranks. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_p)> \mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_q)$$\end{document}, then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_p \succ \mathfrak {s}_q$$\end{document}, meaning that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_p$$\end{document} is preferred over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_q$$\end{document}.

Return: The best site location \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}^* = \arg \max _i \mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_i)$$\end{document} and the objective weight vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{w} = (w_1,\ldots ,w_5)$$\end{document}.

Return: The best site location \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}^* = \arg \max _i \mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}(\mathfrak {s}_i)$$\end{document} and the objective weight vector \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\textbf{w} = (w_1,\ldots ,w_5)$$\end{document}.

Integrated FRI-CRITIC approach for MCDM problems

In fuzzy multi-criteria decision-making, the process of finding the best out of several conflicting criteria is complex. The fuzzy graph provides a flexible modelling structure that bears the intrinsic ambiguity of the relationships among options using membership values. This section provides a systematic approach based on the fuzzy Revan index to evaluate and prioritize options. Suppose that the set of alternatives is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {X} = \{X_1, X_2, \ldots , X_a\}$$\end{document} and the set of criteria is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {Y} = \{Y_1, Y_2, \ldots , Y_b\}$$\end{document}. The weights \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w_j \in [0,1]$$\end{document} are assigned to each criterion, which reflect its relative importance. A fuzzy graph is constructed for every criterion \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y_j$$\end{document} with the vertex set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {V}_{\mathfrak {G}_j} = \mathcal {X}$$\end{document}, representing the alternatives.

The edges \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {x}_p\mathfrak {x}_q \in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_j}$$\end{document} are used to show the strength of influence or similarity between options, measured by the membership function of edge \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu _{\mathfrak {G}_j}(\mathfrak {x}_p\mathfrak {x}_q)$$\end{document}. The dynamic structure of preferences for each criterion is accurately reflected in these graphs. The primary objective is to find the most appropriate alternative, which is the best choice with respect to the most number of criteria, and also plays a significant structural role in the fuzzy network. This includes calculating the fuzzy Revan index for every vertex and summing the scores of the vertices according to their weights across all criteria. The highest total score was chosen as the best score. This approach ensures that relationships are not compromised and that the value of the criteria is considered.

Case study background

To illustrate the practical applicability of the proposed FRI-CRITIC framework, a real-world problem regarding the determination of a highway alignment in Turkey is discussed. Yakar and Celik51 created a three-stage model based on Multi-Criteria Decision Making (MCDM) and Geographical Information Systems (GIS) for the determination of highway alignments in the Tokat region in northern Turkey. This project entailed planning a critical highway alignment in mountainous areas, where the alignment has a strong influence on the cost of construction, repair, and maintenance, operational performance, and environmental and social effects.

The study area covered approximately 150 km in the Tokat province with various topographies, such as mountainous areas, river valleys, and agricultural plains. Fuzzy functions were used to standardize the criteria maps, the Analytic Hierarchy Process (AHP) was used to determine the weights of the criteria, and tthe Weighted Linear Combination method was used to combine the criteria maps using IDRISI Taiga (version 16.0; Clark Labs, Clark University, Worcester, MA, USA; https://clarklabs.org, as reported in the original study by Yakar and Celik51. The entire FRI-CRITIC methodology is illustrated in Fig. 3.Fig. 3Integrated FRI-CRITIC methodology for optimal site selection.

Integrated FRI-CRITIC methodology for optimal site selection.

Fuzzy Revan degree calculation.

Step 7: Fuzzy Revan Index Computation

The FRI is computed using Equation (3).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} FRI(\mathfrak {G}_1)&= \sum _{\mathfrak {u}\mathfrak {v}\in \mathcal {E}_{\mathfrak {G}_1}} \bigl [\mu _{\mathfrak {G}_1}(\mathfrak {u})\,r_{\mathfrak {G}_1}(\mathfrak {u}) + \mu _{\mathfrak {G}_1}(\mathfrak {v})\,r_{\mathfrak {G}_1}(\mathfrak {v})\bigr ] \\&= [0.794 \times 0.70 + 0.377 \times 1.75] + [0.794 \times 0.70 + 0.794 \times 0.55] \\&\quad + [0.377 \times 1.75 + 0.377 \times 1.75] + [0.377 \times 1.75 + 0.794 \times 0.55] \\&\quad + [0.794 \times 0.55 + 0.794 \times 0.55] + [0.645 \times 1.31 + 0.645 \times 1.09] \\&= 11.0260 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 8: Vertex Deletion Contribution Analysis

To quantify the contribution of vertex \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_1$$\end{document}, we remove it and all its incident edges and then recompute the FRI for the reduced graph as follows:15\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} FRI(\mathfrak {G}_1^{\mathfrak {s}_1}) = 1.5620 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}The contribution of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_1$$\end{document} is16\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} FRI_{Y_1}(\mathfrak {s}_1) = FRI(\mathfrak {G}_1) - FRI(\mathfrak {G}_1^{\mathfrak {s}_1}) = 11.0260 - 1.5620 = 9.4640 \end{aligned}$$\end{document}This large positive contribution indicates that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} CRITIC method for objective weights

Step 9: Normalization

The decision matrix is normalized using Equation (8), as follows: For the benefit criteria (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y_3, Y_4, Y_5$$\end{document}):\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{f}_{ij} = \frac{f_{ij} - f_j^-}{f_j^+ - f_j^-} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}For the cost criteria (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$Y_1, Y_2$$\end{document}):\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \tilde{f}_{ij} = \frac{f_j^+ - f_{ij}}{f_j^+ - f_j^-} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 10: Standard Deviation

The standard deviation for each criterion is as follows:17\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \boldsymbol{\sigma } = [\sigma _1\; \sigma _2\; \sigma _3\; \sigma _4\; \sigma _5] = [0.3528\; 0.4349\; 0.5904\; 0.5350\; 0.5605] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 11: Correlation Matrix18\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{R} = \begin{bmatrix} 1.0000 & -0.2154 & 0.3421 & -0.1876 & 0.4231 \\ -0.2154 & 1.0000 & -0.3128 & 0.2563 & -0.1987 \\ 0.3421 & -0.3128 & 1.0000 & -0.2459 & 0.3342 \\ -0.1876 & 0.2563 & -0.2459 & 1.0000 & -0.2765 \\ 0.4231 & -0.1987 & 0.3342 & -0.2765 & 1.0000 \end{bmatrix} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 12: CRITIC Method19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} c_j = \sigma _j \sum _{t=1}^{5} (1 - r_{jt}) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{c} = [1.8193\; 1.6896\; 1.4294\; 1.2603\; 1.9402] \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Step 13: Objective Weights20\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} w_j = \frac{c_j}{\sum _{k=1}^{5} c_k} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \textbf{w} = [0.2235\; The average Spearman correlation of ρ = 0.932 indicates a very strong agreement between the FRI and the five established indices, validating the effectiveness of the proposed approach (Tables 13,14,15,16,17).

Validation with original study findings

The Spearman rank correlation between the FRI-CRITIC ranking and the original study ranking was ρ = 0.943 (p < 0.01), indicating a strong agreement and validating the proposed methodology.

Sensitivity analysis

A thorough sensitivity analysis was performed to test the robustness of the FRI-CRITIC framework by perturbing the weights derived from CRITIC.

Weight perturbation method

Sensitivity analysis: Weight perturbation scenarios.

Robustness index

We define the Robustness Index RI as the percentage of perturbation scenarios in which \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} is still the best choice:24\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} RI = \frac{\text {Number of scenarios where } \mathfrak {s}_3 \text { is optimal}}{\text {Total number of scenarios}} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}In 36 perturbation scenarios, ranging from single and double weight changes to a combination of all 36 weights, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} was optimal in all instances, with a very high level of robustness of the FRI-CRITIC framework.

Superiority of the proposed FRI-CRITIC approach

The proposed FRI-CRITIC approach has several advantages. CRITIC uses the data variance and correlation to calculate weights without the need for subjective pairwise comparisons, thereby avoiding expert bias.The extremal degree information of the network that was missed by the local indices (Zagreb, Randić) is captured by FRI, which leads to a more comprehensive analysis of the network.Existing indices have no ability to quantify the contribution of each alternative, as is provided by the deletion analysis (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G}) - FRI(\mathfrak {G}^{\mathfrak {s}_i})$$\end{document}).\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} is optimal, and the sensitivity analysis shows that it is robust for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pm 30\%$$\end{document} weight perturbations (RI = 100%).The methodology is validated with a strong correlation with the official ranking (0.943) for the first time according to the AHP analysis method of Yakar and Celik.

CRITIC uses the data variance and correlation to calculate weights without the need for subjective pairwise comparisons, thereby avoiding expert bias.

The extremal degree information of the network that was missed by the local indices (Zagreb, Randić) is captured by FRI, which leads to a more comprehensive analysis of the network.

Existing indices have no ability to quantify the contribution of each alternative, as is provided by the deletion analysis (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$FRI(\mathfrak {G}) - FRI(\mathfrak {G}^{\mathfrak {s}_i})$$\end{document}).

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} is optimal, and the sensitivity analysis shows that it is robust for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pm 30\%$$\end{document} weight perturbations (RI = 100%).

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} is optimal, and the sensitivity analysis shows that it is robust for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\pm 30\%$$\end{document} weight perturbations (RI = 100%).

The methodology is validated with a strong correlation with the official ranking (0.943) for the first time according to the AHP analysis method of Yakar and Celik.

The methodology is validated with a strong correlation with the official ranking (0.943) for the first time according to the AHP analysis method of Yakar and Celik.

Conclusion

In this study, a new global structural measure of fuzzy graphs, the Fuzzy Revan Index (FRI), based on extremal fuzzy degrees to measure the deviation type of structural imbalance, is introduced. The FRI was combined with the CRITIC method to quantify the weights in the multi-criteria decision-making process. The proposed framework was applied to a real-world problem of selecting a highway alignment in Tokat, Turkey, based on the data presented by Yakar and Celik (2014), and validated. The FRI was able to correctly identify site \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} (Western hillside route) as the best alignment having the highest score 9.1352.The average Spearman’s rank correlation coefficient between FRI and five existing fuzzy topological indices was found to be ρ = 0.932 which is indicative of good agreement.Correlation of the FRI ranking with the official rank of Yakar and Celik (2014) based on AHP was found as 0.943.Sensitivity analysis showed that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} was also the optimal solution for perturbations with 30% weights, both positive and negative (RI = 100%), meaning that it is robust.Extended the classical Revan index to fuzzy graphs that allows the measurement of structural imbalance under uncertainty.Upper bounds for FRI on paths, cycles, complete graphs, wheel graphs, star graphs and complete bipartite graphs.Created a vertex deletion analysis to compute the individual alternative importance.Integrated FRI with CRITIC for objective weight determination without any subjective expert biases.Provided complete step-by-step computational procedure that allows reader to repeat calculations.Validated with actual case study published for highway in Tokat with official ranking.

The FRI was able to correctly identify site \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} (Western hillside route) as the best alignment having the highest score 9.1352.

The FRI was able to correctly identify site \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} (Western hillside route) as the best alignment having the highest score 9.1352.

The average Spearman’s rank correlation coefficient between FRI and five existing fuzzy topological indices was found to be ρ = 0.932 which is indicative of good agreement.

The average Spearman’s rank correlation coefficient between FRI and five existing fuzzy topological indices was found to be ρ = 0.932 which is indicative of good agreement.

Correlation of the FRI ranking with the official rank of Yakar and Celik (2014) based on AHP was found as 0.943.

Correlation of the FRI ranking with the official rank of Yakar and Celik (2014) based on AHP was found as 0.943.

Sensitivity analysis showed that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} was also the optimal solution for perturbations with 30% weights, both positive and negative (RI = 100%), meaning that it is robust.

Sensitivity analysis showed that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathfrak {s}_3$$\end{document} was also the optimal solution for perturbations with 30% weights, both positive and negative (RI = 100%), meaning that it is robust.

Extended the classical Revan index to fuzzy graphs that allows the measurement of structural imbalance under uncertainty.

Upper bounds for FRI on paths, cycles, complete graphs, wheel graphs, star graphs and complete bipartite graphs.

Limitations

It is important to recognize the following specific limitations. The vertex membership values are sensitive to the parameters α and β in Equation 1. Various settings of the parameters (α from 0.2 to 5, β from 0 to 5) can yield different values for the FRI. Based on empirical calibration, the optimal parameters used were \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha = 5, \beta = 2.5$$\end{document}; general optimal parameters need to be investigated in future studies.The multiplicative operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu (uv) = \mu (u) \cdot \mu (v)$$\end{document} makes the assumption that the memberships of the endpoints are independent. Other operators, such as minimum and t-norms, are more suitable for datasets that are not multiplicative.The vertex deletion analysis takes O(n · |E|) operations. If there are more than \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$10^4$$\end{document} vertices in the graph, the computational costs are high (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$>10^8$$\end{document} operations). For a large number of real-time applications, parallelization or approximation methods are required.The values of FRI are not directly comparable between the different graphs with varying numbers of vertices and edges. Normalization is needed for comparisons across datasets, for example, FRI/|E|.CRITIC assumes that there are linear relationships between criteria. If the datasets have strong nonlinear correlations, other objective weighting methods are used.

The vertex membership values are sensitive to the parameters α and β in Equation 1. Various settings of the parameters (α from 0.2 to 5, β from 0 to 5) can yield different values for the FRI. Based on empirical calibration, the optimal parameters used were \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha = 5, \beta = 2.5$$\end{document}; general optimal parameters need to be investigated in future studies.

The vertex membership values are sensitive to the parameters α and β in Equation 1. Various settings of the parameters (α from 0.2 to 5, β from 0 to 5) can yield different values for the FRI. Based on empirical calibration, the optimal parameters used were \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\alpha = 5, \beta = 2.5$$\end{document}; general optimal parameters need to be investigated in future studies.

The multiplicative operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu (uv) = \mu (u) \cdot \mu (v)$$\end{document} makes the assumption that the memberships of the endpoints are independent. Other operators, such as minimum and t-norms, are more suitable for datasets that are not multiplicative.

The multiplicative operator \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\nu (uv) = \mu (u) \cdot \mu (v)$$\end{document} makes the assumption that the memberships of the endpoints are independent. Other operators, such as minimum and t-norms, are more suitable for datasets that are not multiplicative.

The vertex deletion analysis takes O(n · |E|) operations. If there are more than \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$10^4$$\end{document} vertices in the graph, the computational costs are high (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$>10^8$$\end{document} operations). For a large number of real-time applications, parallelization or approximation methods are required.

The vertex deletion analysis takes O(n · |E|) operations. If there are more than \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$10^4$$\end{document} vertices in the graph, the computational costs are high (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$>10^8$$\end{document} operations). For a large number of real-time applications, parallelization or approximation methods are required.

The values of FRI are not directly comparable between the different graphs with varying numbers of vertices and edges. Normalization is needed for comparisons across datasets, for example, FRI/|E|.

The values of FRI are not directly comparable between the different graphs with varying numbers of vertices and edges. Normalization is needed for comparisons across datasets, for example, FRI/|E|.

CRITIC assumes that there are linear relationships between criteria. If the datasets have strong nonlinear correlations, other objective weighting methods are used.

CRITIC assumes that there are linear relationships between criteria. If the datasets have strong nonlinear correlations, other objective weighting methods are used.

Future directions

Further studies are required to adapt FRI to dynamic fuzzy graphs in real-time network monitoring, examine alternative t-norms for the computation of membership of edges, consider further analysis of large graphs (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n> 10^5$$\end{document}), examine other applications of FRI, such as supply chain risk assessment and social network analysis, and compare CRITIC with other objective weighting methods (entropy, standard deviation) with respect to the FRI framework.
